12. using SDKTemplate.Shared;
13. using System;
14. using Windows.Media.Protection;
15. using Windows.Media.Protection.PlayReady;

17. 
18. 
19. namespace SDKTemplate.ViewModels
20. {
21.     public class ReactiveViewModel : ViewModelBase
22.     {
23.         // The sample is a protected H264 Dash (OnDemand) asset.  
24.         private string moviePath = MainPage.SampleMovieURL;
25.         private string licenseURL = "http://playready.directtaps.net/pr/svc/rightsmanager.asmx?UseSimpleNonPersistentLicense=1";
26. 
27.         public RelayCommand CmdPlayMovie { get; private set; }
28.         public RelayCommand CmdStopMovie { get; private set; }
29. 
30.         // PlayReadyInfo is just used to show PlayReadyStatistics and debug info in the UI 
31.         public PlayReadyInfoViewModel PlayReadyInfo { get; private set; }
32. 
33.         /// <summary>
34.         /// A view model class for the ReactiveRequest Scenario.
35.         /// The ViewModel takes a UI MediaElement in the contructor to wire up commands and events to simplify the sample.
36.         /// Decoupling the MediaElement from the ViewModel would require addtional MVVM infrastucture.
37.         /// </summary>
38.         // TODO[migrate-033]: see PATTERNS.md#controls
39.         public ReactiveViewModel(MediaElement mediaElement)
40.         {
41. 
42.             /// The ProtectionManager provides communication between the player and PlayReady DRM. 
43.             /// The helper class will configure the protection manager for PlayReady and assign an
44.             /// event handler for Service requests.
45.             this.ProtectionManager = PlayReadyHelpers.InitializeProtectionManager(ServiceRequested);
46.             // TODO[migrate-034]: see PATTERNS.md#controls
47.             mediaElement.ProtectionManager = this.ProtectionManager;
48.             /// The PlayReadyInfoViewModel is used in this sample app to show PlayReadyStatistics such as 
49.             /// SecurityLevel and hardware support in the UI.
50.             PlayReadyInfo = new PlayReadyInfoViewModel();
51.             PlayReadyInfo.RefreshStatics();
52. 
53.             /// Reactive license acqusition will happen automatically when setting the source of 
54.             /// a MediaElement to protected content. 
55.             // TODO[migrate-035]: see PATTERNS.md#controls
56.             CmdPlayMovie = new RelayCommand( () => { mediaElement.Source = new Uri(moviePath); SetPlaybackEnabled(true); });
57.             /// The licenseUrl in the sample is set to return a non-peristent license. When there is a 
58.             /// hard Stop() on the playback, a new license will be requested on Play(). 
59.             // TODO[migrate-036]: see PATTERNS.md#controls
60.             CmdStopMovie = new RelayCommand(() => { mediaElement.Stop(); SetPlaybackEnabled(false); });
61. 
62.             // TODO[migrate-037]: see PATTERNS.md#controls
63.             mediaElement.CurrentStateChanged += (s, a) => {
64.                 // TODO[migrate-038]: see PATTERNS.md#controls
65.                 ViewModelBase.Log("Media State::" + mediaElement.CurrentState);
66.             };
67. 
68.             // TODO[migrate-039]: see PATTERNS.md#controls
69.             mediaElement.MediaFailed += (s, a) => {
70.                 ViewModelBase.Log("Err::" + a.ErrorMessage);
71.             };
72.         }
73. 
74. 
75.         /// <summary>
76.         /// The ProtectionManager defers the service call to the ServiceReqested handler.
77.         /// This handler will enable the application to customize the communication (custom data, http headers, manual request)
78.         /// The ServiceCompletion instance will notify the ProtectionManager in the case of queued requests.
79.         /// </summary>
80.         MediaProtectionServiceCompletion serviceCompletionNotifier = null;
81.         void ServiceRequested(MediaProtectionManager sender, ServiceRequestedEventArgs srEvent)
82.         {
83.             serviceCompletionNotifier = srEvent.Completion;
84.             IPlayReadyServiceRequest serviceRequest = (IPlayReadyServiceRequest)srEvent.Request;
85.             ViewModelBase.Log(serviceRequest.GetType().Name);
86.             ProcessServiceRequest(serviceRequest);
87.         }
88. 
89.         /// <summary>
90.         /// The helper class will determine the exact type of ServiceRequest in order to customize and send
91.         /// the service request. ServiceRequests (except for Individualization and Revocation) also support the
92.         /// GenerateManualEnablingChallenge method. This can be used to read and customize the SOAP challenge
93.         /// and manually send the challenge.
94.         /// </summary>
95.         void ProcessServiceRequest(IMediaProtectionServiceRequest serviceRequest)
96.         {
97.             //Alternatively the serviceRequest can be determined by the Guid serviceRequest.Type
98.             if (serviceRequest is PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest)
99.             {
100.                PlayReadyHelpers.ReactiveIndividualization(serviceRequest as PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest, serviceCompletionNotifier, () => PlayReadyInfo.RefreshStatics());
101.                PlayReadyInfo.RefreshStatics();
102.             }
103.             else if (serviceRequest is PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest)
104.             {
105.                 var licenseRequest = serviceRequest as PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest;
106.                 // The inital service request url was taken from the playready header from the dash manifest.
107.                 // This can overridden to a different license service prior to sending the request (staging, production,...). 
108.                 licenseRequest.Uri = new Uri(licenseURL);
109. 
110.                 PlayReadyHelpers.ReactiveLicenseAcquisition(licenseRequest,  serviceCompletionNotifier);
111.                 SetPlaybackEnabled(true);
112.             }
113. 
114.             // Here are the additional service calls available to support other scenarios
115.             //PlayReadySecureStopServiceRequest
116.             //PlayReadyDomainJoinServiceRequest
117.             //PlayReadyDomainLeaveServiceRequest
118.             //PlayReadyMeteringReportServiceRequest
119. 
120.             //PlayReadyRevocationServiceRequest
121. 
122.         }
123. 
124. 
125.         public string MoviePath
126.         {
127.             get
128.             {
129.                 return moviePath;
130.             }
131.             set
132.             {
133.                 if (moviePath != value)
134.                 {
135.                     moviePath = value;
136.                     RaisePropertyChanged();
137.                 }
138.             }
139.         }
140. 
141.         // Method not used in this sample. 
142.         // This shows the minimal configuration for basic reactive playback.
143.         // TODO[migrate-040]: see PATTERNS.md#controls
144.         public void MinConfig(MediaElement mediaElement)
145.         {
146.             var manager = new MediaProtectionManager();
147.             var props = new Windows.Foundation.Collections.PropertySet();
148.             props.Add("{F4637010-03C3-42CD-B932-B48ADF3A6A54}", "Windows.Media.Protection.PlayReady.PlayReadyWinRTTrustedInput");
149.             manager.Properties.Add("Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionSystemIdMapping", props);
150.             manager.Properties.Add("Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionSystemId", "{F4637010-03C3-42CD-B932-B48ADF3A6A54}");
151.             manager.Properties.Add("Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionContainerGuid", "{9A04F079-9840-4286-AB92-E65BE0885F95}");
152. 
153.             MediaProtectionServiceCompletion completionNotifer = null;
154.             manager.ServiceRequested += async (sender, srEvent) =>
155.             {
156.                 completionNotifer = srEvent.Completion;
157.                 var serviceRequest = (IPlayReadyServiceRequest)srEvent.Request;
158.                
159.                 ProcessServiceRequest(serviceRequest);
160.                 if (serviceRequest is PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest)
161.                 {
162.                     var indivRequest = serviceRequest as PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest;
163.                     await indivRequest.BeginServiceRequest();
164.                     serviceCompletionNotifier.Complete(true);
165.                 }
166.                 else if (serviceRequest is PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest)
167.                 {
168.                     var licenseRequest = serviceRequest as PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest;
169.                     //licenseRequest.
170.                     await licenseRequest.BeginServiceRequest();
171.                     serviceCompletionNotifier.Complete(true);
172.                     serviceCompletionNotifier = null;
173.                 }
174. 
175.             };
176.             // TODO[migrate-041]: see PATTERNS.md#controls
177.             mediaElement.ProtectionManager = manager;
178.         }
179.     }
180. }

51. 
52.             // TODO[migrate-028]: see PATTERNS.md#controls
53.             mediaElement.CurrentStateChanged += (s, a) => {
54.                 // TODO[migrate-029]: see PATTERNS.md#controls
55.                 ViewModelBase.Log("Media State::" + mediaElement.CurrentState);
56.             };
57. 
58.             // TODO[migrate-030]: see PATTERNS.md#controls
59.             mediaElement.MediaFailed += (s, a) => {
60.                 ViewModelBase.Log("Media Failed::" + a.ErrorMessage);
61.             };
62. 
63.             /// Proactive license acqusition will ensure a license is available
64.             /// prior to playback
65.             CmdGetLicense = new RelayCommand(() => { GetLicense(new Guid(this.KeyId)); }, () => { return PlayReadyHelpers.IsIndividualized; });
66. 
67.             /// Play is enabled once a license is available
68.             // TODO[migrate-031]: see PATTERNS.md#controls
69.             CmdPlayMovie = new RelayCommand(() => { mediaElement.Source = new Uri(moviePath); SetPlaybackEnabled(true); }, 
70.                                             () => IsLicenseAvailable(new Guid(KeyId)));
71.             // TODO[migrate-032]: see PATTERNS.md#controls
72.             CmdStopMovie = new RelayCommand(() => { mediaElement.Stop(); SetPlaybackEnabled(false); }, 
73.                                             () => IsLicenseAvailable(new Guid(KeyId))); 
74. 
75.             /// Proactive individualization will ensure PlayReady have been configured
76.             /// to begin making license requests
77.             IndividualizeIfNeeded();
78.         }
79. 
80.         /// A check is made to see of the device has been initialize and a indiv
81.         /// service request is sent if not. The callback will notify the app license
82.         /// calls can now be made.
83.         void IndividualizeIfNeeded()
84.         {
85.             if (!PlayReadyHelpers.IsIndividualized)
86.             {
87.                 PlayReadyHelpers.ProactiveIndividualization(() => {
88.                     CmdGetLicense.RaiseCanExecuteChanged();
89.                     PlayReadyInfo.RefreshStatics();
90.                 });
91.             }
92.         }
93. 
94.         /// Tests to see if a license is available for playback.
95.         bool IsLicenseAvailable(Guid kid)
96.         {
97.             // return false is app is not initialized 
98.             // creating a PlayReadyContentHeader in an un-initialized app
99.             // will cause an exception
100.             if (!PlayReadyHelpers.IsIndividualized)
101.             {
102.                 return false;
103.             }
104.             var contentHeader = new PlayReadyContentHeader(kid, "", PlayReadyEncryptionAlgorithm.Aes128Ctr, new Uri(licenseUrl), new Uri(licenseUrl), "", Guid.Empty);
105.             var licenses = new PlayReadyLicenseIterable(contentHeader, false);
106.             foreach (var license in licenses)
107.             {
108.                 if (license.UsableForPlay)
109.                 {
110.                     return true;
111.                 }
112.             }
113. 
114.             return false;
115.         }
116. 
117.         /// A content header is required to make a proactive license request.
118.         /// The header needs at minimum a KeyId and a License URL.
119.         /// Additionally you set a custom data property which will be passed in the signed
120.         /// section of the license request for use by the service.
121.         void GetLicense(Guid kid)
122.         {
123.             var laURL = new Uri(licenseUrl);
124.             var customData = "token:12345";
125.             var contentHeader =  new PlayReadyContentHeader(kid, "", PlayReadyEncryptionAlgorithm.Aes128Ctr, laURL, laURL, customData, Guid.Empty);
126.             PlayReadyHelpers.ProactiveLicenseAcquisition(contentHeader, () => {
127.                 CmdPlayMovie.RaiseCanExecuteChanged();
128.                 CmdStopMovie.RaiseCanExecuteChanged();
129.             });
130.         }
131. 
132.         // The ServiceRequested and ProcessServiceRequest methods are not use in this 
133.         // sample because service calls are made proactively. The app could be re-configured 
134.         // to call Individualization proactively and reactively handle license 
135.         // requests which would used these methods.
136.         MediaProtectionServiceCompletion serviceCompletionNotifier = null;

138.         {

141.             ViewModelBase.Log(serviceRequest.GetType().Name);
142.             ProcessServiceRequest(serviceRequest);
143.         }

146.         {
147.             //Alternatively the serviceRequest can be determined by the Guid serviceRequest.Type
148.             if (serviceRequest is PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest)
149.             {
150.                PlayReadyHelpers.ReactiveIndividualization(serviceRequest as PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest, serviceCompletionNotifier, () => PlayReadyInfo.RefreshStatics());

153.             else if (serviceRequest is PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest)
154.             {
155.                 PlayReadyHelpers.ReactiveLicenseAcquisition(serviceRequest as PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest,  serviceCompletionNotifier);
156.             }
157.         }

82.         {
83.             var localSettings = Windows.Storage.ApplicationData.Current.LocalSettings;
84.             localSettings.CreateContainer("PlayReady", Windows.Storage.ApplicationDataCreateDisposition.Always);

141.         /// The helper class will determine the exact type of ServiceRequest in order to customize and send
142.         /// the service request. ServiceRequests (except for Individualization and Revocation) also support the
143.         /// GenerateManualEnablingChallenge method. This can be used to read and customize the SOAP challenge
144.         /// and manually send the challenge.
145.         /// </summary>
146.         void ProcessServiceRequest(IMediaProtectionServiceRequest serviceRequest)
147.         {
148.             //Alternatively the serviceRequest can be determined by the Guid serviceRequest.Type
149.             if (serviceRequest is PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest)
150.             {
151.                 PlayReadyHelpers.ReactiveIndividualization(serviceRequest as PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest, serviceCompletionNotifier, () => PlayReadyInfo.RefreshStatics());
152.                 PlayReadyInfo.RefreshStatics();
153.             }

155.             {
156.                 var licenseRequest = serviceRequest as PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest;
157.                 // The initial service request url was taken from the playready header from the dash manifest.
158.                 // This can overridden to a different license service prior to sending the request (staging, production,...). 
159.                 licenseRequest.Uri = new Uri(licenseUrl);
160. 
161.                 PlayReadyHelpers.ReactiveLicenseAcquisition(licenseRequest, serviceCompletionNotifier);
162.             }

140.         private async void SendSecureStopRecords()
141.         {
142.             if (publisherCert == null) { return; }

179.         /// <summary>
180.         /// ...
181.         /// </summary>
182.         void ProcessServiceRequest(IMediaProtectionServiceRequest serviceRequest)
183.         {
184.             //Alternatively the serviceRequest can be determined by the Guid serviceRequest.Type
185.             if (serviceRequest is PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest)
186.             {
187.                 PlayReadyHelpers.ReactiveIndividualization(serviceRequest as PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest, serviceCompletionNotifier, () => PlayReadyInfo.RefreshStatics());
188.                 PlayReadyInfo.RefreshStatics();
189.             }
190.             else if (serviceRequest is PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest)
191.             {
192.                 var licenseServiceRequestNotUsed = (serviceRequest as PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest);
193.                 activePlayReadyHeader = licenseServiceRequestNotUsed.ContentHeader;
194. 
195.                 var licenseServiceRequest = licenseSession.CreateLAServiceRequest();
196.                 licenseServiceRequest.ContentHeader = activePlayReadyHeader;
197.                 licenseServiceRequest.Uri = new Uri(licenseUrl);
198.                 PlayReadyHelpers.ReactiveLicenseAcquisition(licenseServiceRequest, serviceCompletionNotifier);
199.             }
200.         }

202.         void RenewActiveLicense()
203.         { 
204.             if (activePlayReadyHeader != null)
205.             {
206.                 var licenseServiceRequest = licenseSession.CreateLAServiceRequest();
207.                 licenseServiceRequest.ContentHeader = activePlayReadyHeader;
208.                 licenseServiceRequest.Uri = new Uri(licenseUrl);
209.                 PlayReadyHelpers.ReactiveLicenseAcquisition(licenseServiceRequest, null);
210.             }
211.         }

213.         private async void GetPublisherCert(string publisherID, Action<byte[]> callback)
214.         {
215.             var localSettings = ApplicationData.Current.LocalSettings;
216.             if (!localSettings.Containers.ContainsKey("PublisherCerts"))
217.             {
218.                 localSettings.CreateContainer("PublisherCerts", Windows.Storage.ApplicationDataCreateDisposition.Always);
219.             }
220. 
221.             if (!localSettings.Containers["PublisherCerts"].Values.ContainsKey(publisherID))
222.             {
223.                 var xDoc = XDocument.Load("Data/MeteringChallenge.xml");
224.                 XNamespace ns = "http://schemas.microsoft.com/DRM/2007/03/protocols";
225.                 xDoc.Descendants(ns + "MID").FirstOrDefault().Value = publisherID;
226. 
227.                 var content = new StringContent(xDoc.ToString(), System.Text.Encoding.UTF8, "text/xml");
228. 
229.                 var client = new HttpClient();
230.                 client.DefaultRequestHeaders.Add("SOAPAction", "http://schemas.microsoft.com/DRM/2007/03/protocols/GetMeteringCertificate");
231.                 var response = await client.PostAsync(secureStopServiceUrl, content);
232. 
233.                 string soapXml = await response.Content.ReadAsStringAsync();
234.                 var xMeteringDoc = XDocument.Parse(soapXml);
235. 
236.                 var base64Cert = xMeteringDoc.Descendants(ns + "MeterCert").FirstOrDefault().Value;
237.                 var cert = Convert.FromBase64String(base64Cert);
238.                 localSettings.Containers["PublisherCerts"].Values[publisherID] = cert;
239.             }
240. 
241.             this.publisherCert = (byte [])localSettings.Containers["PublisherCerts"].Values[publisherID];
242.             callback(this.publisherCert);
243.         }
244.

246.         {
247.             get
248.             {
249.                 return moviePath;
250.             }
251.             set
252.             {
253.                 if (moviePath != value)
254.                 {
255.                     moviePath = value;
256.                     RaisePropertyChanged();
257.                 }
258.             }
259.         }

33.         {
34.             if (this.PropertyChanged != null)

12. using SDKTemplate.ViewModels;
13. using System;
14. using Windows.ApplicationModel.Core;
15. using Windows.Media.Protection;
16. using Windows.Media.Protection.PlayReady;
17. using Windows.UI.Core;
18. 
19. namespace SDKTemplate.Shared
20. {
21.     // This class provides helper methods for common PlayReady operations. 
22.     // Both Reactive and Proactive service call helpers are defined. 
23.     // Proactive helpers will configure a request and use the Reative method for the call.
24.     class PlayReadyHelpers
25.     {
26.         // This property shows if the application has been individualized.
27.         // The PlayReadyCertificateSecurityLevel will be 0 if it has not.
28.         static public bool IsIndividualized
29.         {
30.             get { return PlayReadyStatics.PlayReadyCertificateSecurityLevel != 0; }
31.         }
32. 
33.         static public MediaProtectionManager InitializeProtectionManager(ServiceRequestedEventHandler serviceRequestHandler)
34.         {
35. 
36.             var protectionManager = new MediaProtectionManager();
37. 
38.             //A setting to tell MF that we are using PlayReady.
39.             var props = new Windows.Foundation.Collections.PropertySet();
40.             props.Add("{F4637010-03C3-42CD-B932-B48ADF3A6A54}", "Windows.Media.Protection.PlayReady.PlayReadyWinRTTrustedInput");
41.             protectionManager.Properties.Add("Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionSystemIdMapping", props);
42.             protectionManager.Properties.Add("Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionSystemId", "{F4637010-03C3-42CD-B932-B48ADF3A6A54}");
43.             //Maps the conatiner guid from the manifest or media segment
44.             protectionManager.Properties.Add("Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionContainerGuid", "{9A04F079-9840-4286-AB92-E65BE0885F95}");
45. 
46.             protectionManager.ServiceRequested += new ServiceRequestedEventHandler(serviceRequestHandler);
47.             protectionManager.ComponentLoadFailed += new ComponentLoadFailedEventHandler(ComponentLoadFailed);
48. 
49.             // Windows 10 provides built in support for Dash and does not require additional configuration. 
50.             // This would be be good place to configure a MediaExtensionManager to support another stream source 
51.             // such as the Smooth Streaming SDK
52. 
53.             return protectionManager;
54. 
55.         }
56. 
57.         static public PlayReadyLicenseSession createLicenseSession()
58.         {
59.             //A setting to tell MF that we are using PlayReady.
60.             var propSetMF = new Windows.Foundation.Collections.PropertySet();
61.             propSetMF["Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionSystemId"] = "{F4637010-03C3-42CD-B932-B48ADF3A6A54}";
62.             var cpsystems = new Windows.Foundation.Collections.PropertySet();
63.             cpsystems["{F4637010-03C3-42CD-B932-B48ADF3A6A54}"] = "Windows.Media.Protection.PlayReady.PlayReadyWinRTTrustedInput"; //Playready TrustedInput Class Name
64.             propSetMF["Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionSystemIdMapping"] = cpsystems;
65. 
66.             //Create the MF media session that the license will be tied to
67.             var pmpServer = new Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionPMPServer(propSetMF);
68. 
69.             var propSetPMPServer = new Windows.Foundation.Collections.PropertySet();
70.             //Set the property for the LicenseSession. This tells PlayReady to tie the license to that particular media session
71.             propSetPMPServer["Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionPMPServer"] = pmpServer;
72. 
73.             var licenseSession = new Windows.Media.Protection.PlayReady.PlayReadyLicenseSession(propSetPMPServer);
74.             return licenseSession;
75.         }
76. 
77. 
78.         /// <summary>
79.         /// A ComponentLoadFailed event will occur when binary data fails to load.
80.         /// This an infrequent event that may be triggered by a pending OS update in which DRM compoments 
81.         /// are being renewed and may require a restart.
82.         /// </summary>
83.         static void ComponentLoadFailed(MediaProtectionManager sender, ComponentLoadFailedEventArgs e)
84.         {
85. 
86.             for (int i = 0; i < e.Information.Items.Count; i++)
87.             {
88.                 ViewModelBase.Log(e.Information.Items[i].Name + "\nReasons=0x" + e.Information.Items[i].Reasons + "\n"
89.                                                     + "Renewal Id=" + e.Information.Items[i].RenewalId);
90.             }
91.             e.Completion.Complete(false);
92.         }
93. 
94.         /// <summary>
95.         /// Reactive individualization is triggered by PlayReady when the DRM is first utilized in a new app.
96.         /// If individualization was triggered as part a license request then that request will be queued until
97.         /// the indiv process completes. The serviceCompletionNotifier is utilized to notify the ProtectionManager 
98.         /// that the next service request can begin.
99.         /// </summary>
100.         static public async void ReactiveIndividualization(PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest indivRequest,
101.                                                                 MediaProtectionServiceCompletion serviceCompletionNotifier,
102.                                                                 Action callback = null)
103.         {
104.             Exception exception = null;
105.             try
106.             {
107.                 // The actual service call. 
108.                 // The Indiv ServiceRequest call cannot be customized like most other ServiceRequests. 
109.                 await indivRequest.BeginServiceRequest();
110.             }
111.             catch (Exception ex)
112.             {
113.                 exception = ex;
114.             }
115.             finally
116.             {
117.                 if (serviceCompletionNotifier != null)
118.                 {
119.                     // The Complete call will notify listeners that the service call is complete 
120.                     // and queued service requests can begin.
121.                     serviceCompletionNotifier.Complete(exception == null);
122.                 }
123.                 ViewModelBase.Log("ReactiveIndividualization::Complete");
124.             }
125. 
126.             // The callback is only used in the sample to show PlayReadyStatics within the UI on the 
127.             // completion of the ServiceRequest.
128.             if (callback != null)
129.             {
130.                 var dispatcher = CoreApplication.MainView.CoreWindow.Dispatcher;
131.                 await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
132.                 {
133.                     callback.Invoke();
134.                 });
135.             }
136.         }
137. 
138.         /// <summary>
139.         /// Reactive license acquisition is triggered by PlayReady when the DRM does not have a license for the 
140.         /// content(KeyID) as part of an active playback attempt.
141.         /// Service requests can be queued which is useful in scenarios such as root/leaf license and PlayReady Domains 
142.         /// where one license depends on another. The serviceCompletionNotifier is utilized to notify the ProtectionManager 
143.         /// that the next service request can begin.
144.         /// </summary>
145.         static async public void ReactiveLicenseAcquisition(IPlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest licenseRequest, 
146.                                                                    MediaProtectionServiceCompletion serviceCompletionNotifier,
147.                                                                    Action callback = null)
148.         {
149.             
150.             Exception exception = null;
151.             bool success = false;
152.             try
153.             {
154.                 await licenseRequest.BeginServiceRequest();
155.                 success = true;
156.             }
157.             catch (Exception ex)
158.             {
159.                 exception = ex;
160.             }
161.             finally
162.             {
163.                 if (serviceCompletionNotifier != null)
164.                 {
165.                     // The Complete call will notify listeners that the service call is complete
166.                     // and queued service requests can begin.
167.                     serviceCompletionNotifier.Complete(success);
168.                     serviceCompletionNotifier = null;
169.                 }
170.                 ViewModelBase.Log("ReactiveLicenseAcquisition::Complete");
171.             }
172. 
173.             // optional call back to update UI.
174.             if (callback != null)
175.             {
176.                 var dispatcher = CoreApplication.MainView.CoreWindow.Dispatcher;
177.                 await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
178.                 {
179.                     callback.Invoke();
180.                 });
181. 
182.             }
183.         }
184. 
185.         /// <summary>
186.         /// The individual process can kick off proactively and not need to wait for a reactionary service
187.         /// request from PlayReady. This enables the app to manage when activation occures.
188.         /// </summary>
189.         static public void ProactiveIndividualization(Action callback)
190.         {
191.             //Creating a new Indiv request and using the previous reactive method to complete the request 
192.             var indivRequest = new PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest();
193.             ReactiveIndividualization(indivRequest, null, callback);
194.         }
195. 
196.         /// <summary>
197.         /// The application can proativly request a license by creating a license request and 
198.         /// assigning a header which provides the specifics such as the KeyId of the license needed.
199.         /// </summary>
200.         static public void ProactiveLicenseAcquisition(PlayReadyContentHeader contentHeader, Action callback = null)
201.         {
202.             var laRequest = new PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest();
203.             laRequest.ContentHeader = contentHeader;
204.             ReactiveLicenseAcquisition(laRequest, null, callback);
205.         }
206. 
207. 
208. 
209. 
210.     }
211. }
212.

<a id="threading"></a>
## Threading: CoreDispatcher → DispatcherQueueUWP (replace this):```csharp
await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => StatusText.Text = "Done");
```WinUI 3:```csharp
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() => StatusText.Text = "Done");
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.High, () => ProgressBar.Value = 100);
```Cache the queue off the UI thread via `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()`. UWP's ASTA reentrancy protection is gone — watch for reentrancy in async code that pumps messages. See the official [threading guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/threading).

1. ﻿//*********************************************************
2. //
3. // Copyright (c) Microsoft. All rights reserved.
4. // This code is licensed under the MIT License (MIT).
5. // THIS CODE IS PROVIDED *AS IS* WITHOUT WARRANTY OF
6. // ANY KIND, EITHER EXPRESS OR IMPLIED, INCLUDING ANY
7. // IMPLIED WARRANTIES OF FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR
8. // PURPOSE, MERCHANTABILITY, OR NON-INFRINGEMENT.
9. //
10. //*********************************************************
11. 
12. using System;
13. using System.Collections.Generic;
14. using System.Linq;
15. using System.Text;
16. using System.Threading.Tasks;
17. using System.Windows.Input;
18. 
19. namespace SDKTemplate.Shared
20. {
21.     /// <summary>
22.     /// A command whose sole purpose is to relay its functionality 
23.     /// to other objects by invoking delegates. 
24.     /// The default return value for the CanExecute method is 'true'.
25.     /// <see cref="RaiseCanExecuteChanged"/> needs to be called whenever
26.     /// <see cref="CanExecute"/> is expected to return a different value.
27.     /// </summary>
28.     public class RelayCommand : ICommand
29.     {
30.         private readonly Action _execute;
31.         private readonly Func<bool> _canExecute;
32. 
33.         /// <summary>
34.         /// Raised when RaiseCanExecuteChanged is called.
35.         /// </summary>
36.         public event EventHandler CanExecuteChanged;
37. 
38.         /// <summary>
39.         /// Creates a new command that can always execute.
40.         /// </summary>
41.         /// <param name="execute">The execution logic.</param>
42.         public RelayCommand(Action execute)
43.             : this(execute, null)
44.         {
45.         }
46. 
47.         /// <summary>
48.         /// Creates a new command.
49.         /// </summary>
50.         /// <param name="execute">The execution logic.</param>
51.         /// <param name="canExecute">The execution status logic.</param>
52.         public RelayCommand(Action execute, Func<bool> canExecute)
53.         {
54.             if (execute == null)
55.                 throw new ArgumentNullException("execute");
56.             _execute = execute;
57.             _canExecute = canExecute;
58.         }
59. 
60.         /// <summary>
61.         /// Determines whether this <see cref="RelayCommand"/> can execute in its current state.
62.         /// </summary>
63.         /// <param name="parameter">
64.         /// Data used by the command. If the command does not require data to be passed, this object can be set to null.
65.         /// </param>
66.         /// <returns>true if this command can be executed; otherwise, false.</returns>
67.         public bool CanExecute(object parameter)
68.         {
69.             return _canExecute == null ? true : _canExecute();
70.         }
71. 
72.         /// <summary>
73.         /// Executes the <see cref="RelayCommand"/> on the current command target.
74.         /// </summary>
75.         /// <param name="parameter">
76.         /// Data used by the command. If the command does not require data to be passed, this object can be set to null.
77.         /// </param>
78.         public void Execute(object parameter)
79.         {
80.             _execute();
81.         }
82. 
83.         /// <summary>
84.         /// Method used to raise the <see cref="CanExecuteChanged"/> event
85.         /// to indicate that the return value of the <see cref="CanExecute"/>
86.         /// method has changed.
87.         /// </summary>
88.         public void RaiseCanExecuteChanged()
89.         {
90.             var handler = CanExecuteChanged;
91.             if (handler != null)
92.             {
93.                 handler(this, EventArgs.Empty);
94.             }
95.         }
96.     }
97. }

26.     /// <summary>
27.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
28.     /// </summary>
29.     public sealed partial class MainPage : Page
30.     {
31.         public static MainPage Current;
32.

34.         {
35.             this.InitializeComponent();
36.

45.             // Populate the scenario list from the SampleConfiguration.cs file
46.             var itemCollection = new List<Scenario>();
47.             int i = 1;
48.             foreach (Scenario s in scenarios)
49.             {
50.                 itemCollection.Add(new Scenario { Title = $"{i++}) {s.Title}", ClassType = s.ClassType });
51.             }
52.             ScenarioControl.ItemsSource = itemCollection;
53. 
54.             if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
55.             {
56.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = -1;
57.             }
58.             else
59.             {
60.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = 0;
61.             }
62.         }
63.

65.         /// Called whenever the user changes selection in the scenarios list.  This method will navigate to the respective
66.         /// sample scenario page.
67.         /// </summary>

69.         /// <param name="e"></param>
70.         private void ScenarioControl_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
71.         {
72.             // Clear the status block when navigating scenarios.
73.             NotifyUser(String.Empty, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
74. 
75.             ListBox scenarioListBox = sender as ListBox;
76.             Scenario s = scenarioListBox.SelectedItem as Scenario;
77.             if (s != null)
78.             {
79.                 ScenarioFrame.Navigate(s.ClassType);
80.                 if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
81.                 {
82.                     Splitter.IsPaneOpen = false;
83.                 }
84.             }
85.         }

88.         {
89.             get { return this.scenarios; }
90.         }

92.         /// <summary>
93.         /// Display a message to the user.
94.         /// This method may be called from any thread.
95.         /// </summary>
96.         /// <param name="strMessage"></param>
97.         /// <param name="type"></param>
98.         public void NotifyUser(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
99.         {
100.             // If called from the UI thread, then update immediately.
101.             // Otherwise, schedule a task on the UI thread to perform the update.
102.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
103.             {
104.                 UpdateStatus(strMessage, type);
105.             }
106.             else
107.             {
108.                 var task = Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => UpdateStatus(strMessage, type));
109.             }
110.         }
111. 
112.         private void UpdateStatus(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
113.         {
114.             switch (type)
115.             {
116.                 case NotifyType.StatusMessage:
117.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Green);
118.                     break;
119.                 case NotifyType.ErrorMessage:
120.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Red);
121.                     break;
122.             }
123. 
124.             StatusBlock.Text = strMessage;
125. 
126.             // Collapse the StatusBlock if it has no text to conserve real estate.
127.             StatusBorder.Visibility = (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty) ? Visibility.Visible : Visibility.Collapsed;
128.             if (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty)
129.             {
130.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
131.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
132.             }
133.             else
134.             {
135.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
136.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
137.             }
138. 
139. 			// Raise an event if necessary to enable a screen reader to announce the status update.
140. 			var peer = FrameworkElementAutomationPeer.FromElement(StatusBlock);
141. 			if (peer != null)
142. 			{
143. 				peer.RaiseAutomationEvent(AutomationEvents.LiveRegionChanged);
144. 			}
145. 		}
146.

148.         {
149.             await Windows.System.Launcher.LaunchUriAsync(new Uri(((HyperlinkButton)sender).Tag.ToString()));
150.         }

154.             Splitter.IsPaneOpen = !Splitter.IsPaneOpen;
155.         }

<a id="csproj"></a>
## Project File Updates- Target a current WinAppSDK-supported TFM. The exact TFM is the source of truth in the project's `.csproj`; do not hard-code it across instruction files. Typical values at time of writing:
  - `net8.0-windows10.0.19041.0` (LTS)
  - `net9.0-windows10.0.19041.0`
  - `net10.0-windows10.0.26100.0` (current `dotnet new winui` default in this repo)
- Add `<UseWinUI>true</UseWinUI>`.
- Add `<EnableMsixTooling>true</EnableMsixTooling>` for packaged builds.
- Reference `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK` and `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools`.
- Reference `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools.WinApp` to wire `dotnet run` into `winapp run`.
- Keep `Package.appxmanifest` for packaged scenarios; set `<WindowsPackageType>None</WindowsPackageType>` for unpackaged.### PackageReference reconciliation cheat-sheet`Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1` preserves the UWP `.csproj` at `<Target>/.uwp-source/` and leaves the WinUI 3 scaffold's `.csproj` intact. Open both side-by-side and merge:- **Drop** (UWP-only — never carry over):
  - `Microsoft.NETCore.UniversalWindowsPlatform`
  - Any `<PackageReference>` whose version string contains `uap` or whose name starts with `Windows.SDK.Contracts`.
- **Keep** (already in the scaffold — do not duplicate or downgrade):
  - `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK`
  - `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools`
- **Add** (project-specific — copy across, then update to a WinAppSDK-compatible version):
  - Third-party libraries the UWP project pulled in (`CommunityToolkit.*`, `Microsoft.Extensions.*`, `Newtonsoft.Json`, etc.). Pick the latest stable release; UWP-pinned versions are usually too old.Do **not** copy the UWP `.csproj` over the scaffold's. The two formats are incompatible — the UWP csproj carries `<TargetPlatformIdentifier>UAP</TargetPlatformIdentifier>`, `<OutputType>AppContainerExe</OutputType>`, explicit `<Compile Include="...">` items, and `Microsoft.Common.props` imports, none of which build under WinAppSDK.### Package.appxmanifest — reconcile image references with the assets you actually haveThe WinUI 3 scaffold ships with a default `Package.appxmanifest` that references assets the template provides under `Assets/` (`SplashScreen.scale-200.png`, `Square150x150Logo.scale-200.png`, `Square44x44Logo.scale-200.png`, `StoreLogo.png`, `Wide310x150Logo.scale-200.png`, `LockScreenLogo.scale-200.png`). The UWP SDK sample's manifest usually points at sample-branded assets with a `-sdk` suffix (`Splash-sdk.png`, `squareTile-sdk.png`, `SmallTile-sdk.png`, `StoreLogo-sdk.png`).If you copy the UWP manifest's `Logo` / `Square150x150Logo` / `Square44x44Logo` / `<uap:SplashScreen Image="...">` / `<uap:DefaultTile Wide310x150Logo="...">` references over the scaffold's without also bringing the matching files, **`winapp run` will register the package but the AppX layout will fail to launch** with errors like:```
AppxManifest.xml(...): error 0x80070002: ... splash screen image [Splash-sdk.png] cannot be located.
```(Failure code on the AppX side is typically `0x80073CF6` — "package could not be registered".)You have two options. Pick one **before** the first `winapp run`:- **Preferred — keep the scaffold's manifest image references** (`Assets\SplashScreen.scale-200.png`, etc.). When you merge any other content from the UWP manifest into the scaffold's (capabilities, file-type associations, `<uap:Extension>`, `<Application Id>`, etc.), do **not** overwrite the `Logo`, `<uap:SplashScreen Image>`, `Square150x150Logo`, `Square44x44Logo`, or `Wide310x150Logo` attribute values. The scaffold's defaults already match the files in its `Assets/` folder.- **Alternative — keep the UWP sample's branded assets.** Copy every file the UWP manifest references from `.uwp-source/Assets/` (or `.uwp-source/<SampleName>/Assets/`) into the new project's `Assets/` folder, preserving the exact filename (including the `-sdk` suffix and any scale qualifiers). Verify by re-reading each `Image=`/`Logo>` value in the manifest and confirming `Test-Path "Assets\<that filename>"` for every one of them. Don't rename files to look prettier — the manifest reference is the source of truth.Either way, before the first `winapp run`, sanity-check every asset reference in the manifest resolves:

```powershell
[xml]$m = Get-Content .\Package.appxmanifest
@($m.Package.Properties.Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.Square150x150Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.Square44x44Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.uap_SplashScreen.Image,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.uap_DefaultTile.Wide310x150Logo
) | Where-Object { $_ } | ForEach-Object {
    if (-not (Test-Path $_)) { Write-Host "[MISSING] $_" -ForegroundColor Red }
}
```

### Manifest migration checklist (Windows.Desktop + runFullTrust)

The default UWP `Package.appxmanifest` declares itself as a Universal app, but a packaged WinUI 3 desktop app is a Win32
2 process with package identity — it needs a different shape. `winapp run` will register the AppX but **fail to deploy** 
 with `0x80073CF6` or "requires runFullTrust capability" if any of the following are missing.

When merging the UWP manifest into the scaffold's, make sure all of these are true:

1. **TargetDeviceFamily is `Windows.Desktop`** — not `Windows.Universal`:
   ```xml
   <Dependencies>
     <TargetDeviceFamily Name="Windows.Desktop" MinVersion="10.0.17763.0" MaxVersionTested="10.0.22621.0" />
   </Dependencies>
   ```
   Windows.Universal is UWP-only and rejected for a Win32 entrypoint.

2. **rescap namespace declared on `<Package>` and added to `IgnorableNamespaces`**:
   ```xml
   <Package
     xmlns="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/foundation/windows10"
     xmlns:uap="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/uap/windows10"
     xmlns:rescap="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/foundation/windows10/restrictedcapabilities"
     IgnorableNamespaces="uap rescap">
   ```
   Without the namespace declaration, item 3 below is silently stripped by AppX validation and you get the "requires run
nFullTrust" error at deploy time.

3. **`runFullTrust` capability declared**:
   ```xml
   <Capabilities>
     <rescap:Capability Name="runFullTrust" />
   </Capabilities>
   ```
   Packaged WinUI 3 desktop apps run outside the UWP AppContainer sandbox and must declare this. Keep any UWP `<Capabili
ity>` entries you actually use (e.g. `<DeviceCapability Name="webcam" />`) but add the `runFullTrust` line above no matte
er what.

4. **`<Application EntryPoint="$targetentrypoint$">`** — the WinUI 3 scaffold uses an MSBuild placeholder that the build
d resolves to the real entry point. Don't replace it with a literal `<UwpAppName>.App` (that's a UWP entry-point pattern)
).

5. **Reconcile UWP `<Extension>` blocks — especially `windows.backgroundTasks`.** The checklist above is *not* complete 
 once items 1–4 pass: a copied UWP manifest often carries `<Extension>` declarations that are invalid in a packaged WinUI
I 3 manifest. The most common and most damaging is an in-process background task:
   ```xml
   <Extensions>
     <Extension Category="windows.backgroundTasks" EntryPoint="BackgroundTask.GeofenceBackgroundTask">
       <BackgroundTasks><Task Type="location" /></BackgroundTasks>
     </Extension>
   </Extensions>
   ```
   UWP auto-registered these WinRT classes; a **packaged WinUI 3 / Windows App SDK** app does not. At launch the AppX re
egistrar rejects the *entire* package with:
   ```
   error 0x80080204: App manifest validation error … not allowed to have
   EntryPoint="…" without ActivatableClassId in windows.activatableClass.inProcessServer
   ```
   The **build stays clean**, so this only appears at `winapp run` / `dotnet run` time and otherwise silently zeroes the
e score (the app never launches, every scenario is unreachable). Two fixes:
   - **Default (recommended for foreground parity):** delete the `<Extension Category="windows.backgroundTasks">` block(
(s). The C# `BackgroundTaskBuilder` registration code still compiles; only the in-app `Register()` call is a no-op / thro
ows (already inside a scenario handler), and every *foreground* scenario becomes reachable. Parity checking exercises the
e foreground UI, not the background trigger, so this restores a launchable app with no scenario loss.
   - **Full fidelity (only if background triggers are in scope):** add a matching `<Extension Category="windows.activata
ableClass.inProcessServer">` with an `<ActivatableClass ActivatableClassId="…">` entry for each task's EntryPoint.       

   `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` Section 5c flags this statically and FAILs before the smoke launch, since the registratio
on failure is deterministic.

### WUI analyzer warnings (UWP API residue)

The benchmark's `winapp build` injects the `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK.Analyzers` package, which flags UWP-only APIs that c
compile cleanly under WinUI 3 but throw `COMException` at runtime — typically inside `Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Application.Start
t(...)` before any window can render. The runner sees this as `builds=true, runs=false`, and `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` 
 will FAIL the build healthcheck for each unique warning.

| Rule | Symptom | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `WUI0002` | `Window.Current does not exist in WinUI 3 desktop apps` | Store the `Window` reference in `App.xaml.cs` (`
`App.Window`) and pass it where needed; see "Windowing" above. |
| `WUI0003` | `CoreDispatcher is UWP-only` | Use `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()` and `TryEnqueue(...)`; see "Thr
reading" above. |
| `WUI0004` | `SystemNavigationManager.GetForCurrentView() is UWP-only` | Drop the system back button hookup, or use HWN
ND-based COM interop; see "GetForCurrentView Replacements" above. |

Treat every `warning WUI000\d` line in the build output as a defect — the analyzer does not produce false positives. Sea
arch for the API name in this document for the recommended replacement.

### Bootstrap-neutralized helper classes (filter-prone)

Some SDK Samples ship a shared `Common\NavigationHelper.cs` containing a `RootFrameNavigationHelper` class that wires AL
LT+Left / BrowserBack (virtual key 166) / mouse XButton1+XButton2 to frame back/forward navigation. Its WinUI 3 equivalen
nt requires `Microsoft.UI.Input.InputKeyboardSource.GetKeyState` or P/Invoke `GetAsyncKeyState` plus virtual-key code con
nstants — exactly the shape the model provider's content-safety filter classifies as "keylogger / surveillance" code. Mig
grating it routinely produces `CAPIError: 400 Output blocked by content filtering policy`.

`Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1` handles this automatically: before the agent ever sees the file, the bootstrap rewrites ju
ust the `RootFrameNavigationHelper` class body to a no-op stub (preserving the public ctor with `params object[] args` so
o callers compile no matter what signature they invoke). The audit log records `NEUTRALIZED  class RootFrameNavigationHel
lper`. The mapping row's Notes column is prefixed with `NEUTRALIZED:`.

**You don't need to migrate the neutralized class.** Just leave both it and the call site (typically `new RootFrameNavig
gationHelper(rootFrame)` in `App.xaml.cs`'s `OnLaunched`) untouched — back-nav is not a demonstrated feature of any UWP S
SDK sample. The remainder of `NavigationHelper.cs` (the `NavigationHelper` class used for per-page state save/restore, th
he LoadStateEventArgs / SaveStateEventArgs types) is migrated normally; it does not trigger the filter.

If you really want ALT+Left back-nav, add a single declarative XAML element to the AppBarButton that triggers GoBack:   

```xml
<AppBarButton Icon="Back" Click="OnBackClick">
  <AppBarButton.KeyboardAccelerators>
    <KeyboardAccelerator Key="Left" Modifiers="Menu"/>
  </AppBarButton.KeyboardAccelerators>
</AppBarButton>
```

`KeyboardAccelerator` is declarative and does not trip the filter.
<exited with exit code 0>

null;\n            bool success = false;\n            try\n            {\n                await licenseRequest.BeginServiceRequest();\n                success = true;\n            }\n            catch (Exception ex)\n            {\n                exception = ex;\n            }\n            finally\n            {\n                if (serviceCompletionNotifier != null)\n                {\n                    serviceCompletionNotifier.Complete(success);\n                    serviceCompletionNotifier = null;\n                }\n                ViewModelBase.Log(\"ReactiveLicenseAcquisition::Complete\");\n            }\n\n            if (callback != null)\n            {\n                ViewModelBase.MainDispatcherQueue?.TryEnqueue(() =>\n                {\n                    callback.Invoke();\n                });\n            }\n        }\n\n        static public void ProactiveIndividualization(Action callback)\n        {\n       {\n            if (serviceRequest is PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest)\n            {\n                PlayReadyHelpers.ReactiveIndividualization(serviceRequest as PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest, serviceCompletionNotifier, () => PlayReadyInfo.RefreshStatics());\n                PlayReadyInfo.RefreshStatics();\n            }\n            else if (serviceRequest is PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest)\n            {\n                var licenseServiceRequestNotUsed = (serviceRequest as PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest);\n                activePlayReadyHeader = licenseServiceRequestNotUsed.ContentHeader;\n\n                var licenseServiceRequest = licenseSession.CreateLAServiceRequest();\n                licenseServiceRequest.ContentHeader = activePlayReadyHeader;\n                licenseServiceRequest.Uri = new Uri(licenseUrl);\n                PlayReadyHelpers.ReactiveLicenseAcquisition(licenseServiceRequest, serviceCompletionNotifier);\n            }\n        }\n\n        void RenewActiveLicense()\n        {\n            if (activePlayReadyHeader != null)\n            {\n                var licenseServiceRequest = licenseSession.CreateLAServiceRequest();\n                licenseServiceRequest.ContentHeader = activePlayReadyHeader;\n                licenseServiceRequest.Uri = new Uri(licenseUrl);\n                PlayReadyHelpers.ReactiveLicenseAcquisition(licenseServiceRequest, null);\n            }\n        }\n\n        private async void GetPublisherCert(string publisherID, Action<byte[]> callback)\n        {\n            var localSettings = ApplicationData.Current.LocalSettings;\n            if (!localSettings.Containers.ContainsKey(\"PublisherCerts\"))\n            {\n                localSettings.CreateContainer(\"PublisherCerts\", Windows.Storage.ApplicationDataCreateDisposition.Always);\n            }\n\n            if (!localSettings.Containers[\"PublisherCerts\"].Values.ContainsKey(publisherID))\n            {\n                var xDoc = XDocument.Load(\"Data/MeteringChallenge.xml\");\n                XNamespace ns = \"http://schemas.microsoft.com/DRM/2007/03/protocols\";\n                xDoc.Descendants(ns + \"MID\").FirstOrDefault().Value = publisherID;\n\n                var content = new StringContent(xDoc.ToString(), System.Text.Encoding.UTF8, \"text/xml\");\n\n                var client = new HttpClient();\n                client.DefaultRequestHeaders.Add(\"SOAPAction\", \"http://schemas.microsoft.com/DRM/2007/03/protocols/GetMeteringCertificate\");\n                var response = await client.PostAsync(secureStopServiceUrl, content);\n\n                string soapXml = await response.Content.ReadAsStringAsync();\n                var xMeteringDoc = XDocument.Parse(soapXml);\n\n                var base64Cert = xMeteringDoc.Descendants(ns + \"MeterCert\").FirstOrDefault().Value;\n                var cert = Convert.FromBase64String(base64Cert);\n                localSettings.Containers[\"PublisherCerts\"].Values[publisherID] = cert;\n            }\n\n            this.publisherCert = (byte[])localSettings.Containers[\"PublisherCerts\"].Values[publisherID];\n            callback(this.publisherCert);\n        }\n\n        public string MoviePath\n        {\n            get { return moviePath; }\n            set\n            {\n                if (moviePath != value)\n                {\n                    moviePath = value;\n                    RaisePropertyChanged();\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n### 17. 23.     public class ViewModelBase : INotifyPropertyChanged

26.         public event PropertyChangedEventHandler PropertyChanged;
27.         /// <summary>
28.         /// Fires an event when called. Used to update the UI in the MVVM world.
29.         /// [CallerMemberName] Ensures only the peoperty that calls it gets the event
30.         /// and not every property
31.         /// </summary>
32.         protected void RaisePropertyChanged([CallerMemberName] string propertyName = "")
33.         {
34.             if (this.PropertyChanged != null)
35.             {
36.                 this.PropertyChanged(this, new PropertyChangedEventArgs(propertyName));
37.             }
38.         }
39. 
40.         public async static void Log(string message)
41.         {
42.             var dispatcher = CoreApplication.MainView.CoreWindow.Dispatcher;
43.             await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
44.             {
45.                 outputLog.Insert(0, message);
46.             });
47.         }
48.

50.         {
51.             var dispatcher = CoreApplication.MainView.CoreWindow.Dispatcher;
52.             await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
53.             {
54.                 PlaybackEnabled = enabled;
55.             });
56.         }

12. using SDKTemplate.ViewModels;
13. using System;
14. using Windows.ApplicationModel.Core;
15. using Windows.Media.Protection;
16. using Windows.Media.Protection.PlayReady;
17. using Windows.UI.Core;
18. 
19. namespace SDKTemplate.Shared
20. {
21.     // This class provides helper methods for common PlayReady operations. 
22.     // Both Reactive and Proactive service call helpers are defined. 
23.     // Proactive helpers will configure a request and use the Reative method for the call.
24.     class PlayReadyHelpers
25.     {
26.         // This property shows if the application has been individualized.
27.         // The PlayReadyCertificateSecurityLevel will be 0 if it has not.
28.         static public bool IsIndividualized
29.         {
30.             get { return PlayReadyStatics.PlayReadyCertificateSecurityLevel != 0; }
31.         }
32. 
33.         static public MediaProtectionManager InitializeProtectionManager(ServiceRequestedEventHandler serviceRequestHandler)
34.         {
35. 
36.             var protectionManager = new MediaProtectionManager();
37. 
38.             //A setting to tell MF that we are using PlayReady.
39.             var props = new Windows.Foundation.Collections.PropertySet();
40.             props.Add("{F4637010-03C3-42CD-B932-B48ADF3A6A54}", "Windows.Media.Protection.PlayReady.PlayReadyWinRTTrustedInput");
41.             protectionManager.Properties.Add("Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionSystemIdMapping", props);
42.             protectionManager.Properties.Add("Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionSystemId", "{F4637010-03C3-42CD-B932-B48ADF3A6A54}");
43.             //Maps the conatiner guid from the manifest or media segment
44.             protectionManager.Properties.Add("Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionContainerGuid", "{9A04F079-9840-4286-AB92-E65BE0885F95}");
45. 
46.             protectionManager.ServiceRequested += new ServiceRequestedEventHandler(serviceRequestHandler);
47.             protectionManager.ComponentLoadFailed += new ComponentLoadFailedEventHandler(ComponentLoadFailed);
48. 
49.             // Windows 10 provides built in support for Dash and does not require additional configuration. 
50.             // This would be be good place to configure a MediaExtensionManager to support another stream source 
51.             // such as the Smooth Streaming SDK
52. 
53.             return protectionManager;
54. 
55.         }
56. 
57.         static public PlayReadyLicenseSession createLicenseSession()
58.         {
59.             //A setting to tell MF that we are using PlayReady.
60.             var propSetMF = new Windows.Foundation.Collections.PropertySet();
61.             propSetMF["Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionSystemId"] = "{F4637010-03C3-42CD-B932-B48ADF3A6A54}";
62.             var cpsystems = new Windows.Foundation.Collections.PropertySet();
63.             cpsystems["{F4637010-03C3-42CD-B932-B48ADF3A6A54}"] = "Windows.Media.Protection.PlayReady.PlayReadyWinRTTrustedInput"; //Playready TrustedInput Class Name
64.             propSetMF["Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionSystemIdMapping"] = cpsystems;
65. 
66.             //Create the MF media session that the license will be tied to
67.             var pmpServer = new Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionPMPServer(propSetMF);
68. 
69.             var propSetPMPServer = new Windows.Foundation.Collections.PropertySet();
70.             //Set the property for the LicenseSession. This tells PlayReady to tie the license to that particular media session
71.             propSetPMPServer["Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionPMPServer"] = pmpServer;
72. 
73.             var licenseSession = new Windows.Media.Protection.PlayReady.PlayReadyLicenseSession(propSetPMPServer);
74.             return licenseSession;
75.         }
76. 
77. 
78.         /// <summary>
79.         /// A ComponentLoadFailed event will occur when binary data fails to load.
80.         /// This an infrequent event that may be triggered by a pending OS update in which DRM compoments 
81.         /// are being renewed and may require a restart.
82.         /// </summary>
83.         static void ComponentLoadFailed(MediaProtectionManager sender, ComponentLoadFailedEventArgs e)
84.         {
85. 
86.             for (int i = 0; i < e.Information.Items.Count; i++)
87.             {
88.                 ViewModelBase.Log(e.Information.Items[i].Name + "\nReasons=0x" + e.Information.Items[i].Reasons + "\n"
89.                                                     + "Renewal Id=" + e.Information.Items[i].RenewalId);
90.             }
91.             e.Completion.Complete(false);
92.         }
93. 
94.         /// <summary>
95.         /// Reactive individualization is triggered by PlayReady when the DRM is first utilized in a new app.
96.         /// If individualization was triggered as part a license request then that request will be queued until
97.         /// the indiv process completes. The serviceCompletionNotifier is utilized to notify the ProtectionManager 
98.         /// that the next service request can begin.
99.         /// </summary>
100.         static public async void ReactiveIndividualization(PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest indivRequest,
101.                                                                 MediaProtectionServiceCompletion serviceCompletionNotifier,
102.                                                                 Action callback = null)
103.         {
104.             Exception exception = null;
105.             try
106.             {
107.                 // The actual service call. 
108.                 // The Indiv ServiceRequest call cannot be customized like most other ServiceRequests. 
109.                 await indivRequest.BeginServiceRequest();
110.             }
111.             catch (Exception ex)
112.             {
113.                 exception = ex;
114.             }
115.             finally
116.             {
117.                 if (serviceCompletionNotifier != null)
118.                 {
119.                     // The Complete call will notify listeners that the service call is complete 
120.                     // and queued service requests can begin.
121.                     serviceCompletionNotifier.Complete(exception == null);
122.                 }
123.                 ViewModelBase.Log("ReactiveIndividualization::Complete");
124.             }
125. 
126.             // The callback is only used in the sample to show PlayReadyStatics within the UI on the 
127.             // completion of the ServiceRequest.
128.             if (callback != null)
129.             {
130.                 var dispatcher = CoreApplication.MainView.CoreWindow.Dispatcher;
131.                 await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
132.                 {
133.                     callback.Invoke();
134.                 });
135.             }
136.         }
137. 
138.         /// <summary>
139.         /// Reactive license acquisition is triggered by PlayReady when the DRM does not have a license for the 
140.         /// content(KeyID) as part of an active playback attempt.
141.         /// Service requests can be queued which is useful in scenarios such as root/leaf license and PlayReady Domains 
142.         /// where one license depends on another. The serviceCompletionNotifier is utilized to notify the ProtectionManager 
143.         /// that the next service request can begin.
144.         /// </summary>
145.         static async public void ReactiveLicenseAcquisition(IPlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest licenseRequest, 
146.                                                                    MediaProtectionServiceCompletion serviceCompletionNotifier,
147.                                                                    Action callback = null)
148.         {
149.             
150.             Exception exception = null;
151.             bool success = false;
152.             try
153.             {
154.                 await licenseRequest.BeginServiceRequest();
155.                 success = true;
156.             }
157.             catch (Exception ex)
158.             {
159.                 exception = ex;
160.             }
161.             finally
162.             {
163.                 if (serviceCompletionNotifier != null)
164.                 {
165.                     // The Complete call will notify listeners that the service call is complete
166.                     // and queued service requests can begin.
167.                     serviceCompletionNotifier.Complete(success);
168.                     serviceCompletionNotifier = null;
169.                 }
170.                 ViewModelBase.Log("ReactiveLicenseAcquisition::Complete");
171.             }
172. 
173.             // optional call back to update UI.
174.             if (callback != null)
175.             {
176.                 var dispatcher = CoreApplication.MainView.CoreWindow.Dispatcher;
177.                 await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
178.                 {
179.                     callback.Invoke();
180.                 });
181. 
182.             }
183.         }
184. 
185.         /// <summary>
186.         /// The individual process can kick off proactively and not need to wait for a reactionary service
187.         /// request from PlayReady. This enables the app to manage when activation occures.
188.         /// </summary>
189.         static public void ProactiveIndividualization(Action callback)
190.         {
191.             //Creating a new Indiv request and using the previous reactive method to complete the request 
192.             var indivRequest = new PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest();
193.             ReactiveIndividualization(indivRequest, null, callback);
194.         }
195. 
196.         /// <summary>
197.         /// The application can proativly request a license by creating a license request and 
198.         /// assigning a header which provides the specifics such as the KeyId of the license needed.
199.         /// </summary>
200.         static public void ProactiveLicenseAcquisition(PlayReadyContentHeader contentHeader, Action callback = null)
201.         {
202.             var laRequest = new PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest();
203.             laRequest.ContentHeader = contentHeader;
204.             ReactiveLicenseAcquisition(laRequest, null, callback);
205.         }
206. 
207. 
208. 
209. 
210.     }
211. }

32.             protectionManager.Properties.Add("Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionSystemIdMapping", props);
33.             protectionManager.Properties.Add("Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionSystemId", "{F4637010-03C3-42CD-B932-B48ADF3A6A54}");
34.             protectionManager.Properties.Add("Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionContainerGuid", "{9A04F079-9840-4286-AB92-E65BE0885F95}");
35.

149. namespace SDKTemplate.Shared
150. {
151.     // This class provides helper methods for common PlayReady operations. 
152.     // Both Reactive and Proactive service call helpers are defined. 
153.     // Proactive helpers will configure a request and use the Reative method for the call.
154.     class PlayReadyHelpers
155.     {
156.         // This property shows if the application has been individualized.
157.         // The PlayReadyCertificateSecurityLevel will be 0 if it has not.
158.         static public bool IsIndividualized
159.         {
160.             get { return PlayReadyStatics.PlayReadyCertificateSecurityLevel != 0; }
161.         }
162. 
163.         static public MediaProtectionManager InitializeProtectionManager(ServiceRequestedEventHandler serviceRequestHandler)
164.         {
165. 
166.             var protectionManager = new MediaProtectionManager();
167. 
168.             //A setting to tell MF that we are using PlayReady.
169.             var props = new Windows.Foundation.Collections.PropertySet();
170.             props.Add("{F4637010-03C3-42CD-B932-B48ADF3A6A54}", "Windows.Media.Protection.PlayReady.PlayReadyWinRTTrustedInput");
171.             protectionManager.Properties.Add("Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionSystemIdMapping", props);
172.             protectionManager.Properties.Add("Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionSystemId", "{F4637010-03C3-42CD-B932-B48ADF3A6A54}");
173.             //Maps the conatiner guid from the manifest or media segment
174.             protectionManager.Properties.Add("Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionContainerGuid", "{9A04F079-9840-4286-AB92-E65BE0885F95}");
175. 
176.             protectionManager.ServiceRequested += new ServiceRequestedEventHandler(serviceRequestHandler);
177.             protectionManager.ComponentLoadFailed += new ComponentLoadFailedEventHandler(ComponentLoadFailed);
178. 
179.             // Windows 10 provides built in support for Dash and does not require additional configuration. 
180.             // This would be be good place to configure a MediaExtensionManager to support another stream source 
181.             // such as the Smooth Streaming SDK
182. 
183.             return protectionManager;
184. 
185.         }
186. 
187.         static public PlayReadyLicenseSession createLicenseSession()
188.         {
189.             //A setting to tell MF that we are using PlayReady.
190.             var propSetMF = new Windows.Foundation.Collections.PropertySet();
191.             propSetMF["Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionSystemId"] = "{F4637010-03C3-42CD-B932-B48ADF3A6A54}";
192.             var cpsystems = new Windows.Foundation.Collections.PropertySet();
193.             cpsystems["{F4637010-03C3-42CD-B932-B48ADF3A6A54}"] = "Windows.Media.Protection.PlayReady.PlayReadyWinRTTrustedInput"; //Playready TrustedInput Class Name
194.             propSetMF["Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionSystemIdMapping"] = cpsystems;
195. 
196.             //Create the MF media session that the license will be tied to
197.             var pmpServer = new Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionPMPServer(propSetMF);
198. 
199.             var propSetPMPServer = new Windows.Foundation.Collections.PropertySet();
200.             //Set the property for the LicenseSession. This tells PlayReady to tie the license to that particular media session
201.             propSetPMPServer["Windows.Media.Protection.MediaProtectionPMPServer"] = pmpServer;
202. 
203.             var licenseSession = new Windows.Media.Protection.PlayReady.PlayReadyLicenseSession(propSetPMPServer);
204.             return licenseSession;
205.         }
206. 
207. 
208.         /// <summary>
209.         /// A ComponentLoadFailed event will occur when binary data fails to load.
210.         /// This an infrequent event that may be triggered by a pending OS update in which DRM compoments 
211.         /// are being renewed and may require a restart.
212.         /// </summary>
213.         static void ComponentLoadFailed(MediaProtectionManager sender, ComponentLoadFailedEventArgs e)
214.         {
215. 
216.             for (int i = 0; i < e.Information.Items.Count; i++)
217.             {
218.                 ViewModelBase.Log(e.Information.Items[i].Name + "\nReasons=0x" + e.Information.Items[i].Reasons + "\n"
219.                                                     + "Renewal Id=" + e.Information.Items[i].RenewalId);
220.             }
221.             e.Completion.Complete(false);
222.         }
223. 
224.         /// <summary>
225.         /// Reactive individualization is triggered by PlayReady when the DRM is first utilized in a new app.
226.         /// If individualization was triggered as part a license request then that request will be queued until
227.         /// the indiv process completes. The serviceCompletionNotifier is utilized to notify the ProtectionManager 
228.         /// that the next service request can begin.
229.         /// </summary>
230.         static public async void ReactiveIndividualization(PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest indivRequest,
231.                                                                 MediaProtectionServiceCompletion serviceCompletionNotifier,
232.                                                                 Action callback = null)
233.         {
234.             Exception exception = null;
235.             try
236.             {
237.                 // The actual service call. 
238.                 // The Indiv ServiceRequest call cannot be customized like most other ServiceRequests. 
239.                 await indivRequest.BeginServiceRequest();
240.             }
241.             catch (Exception ex)
242.             {
243.                 exception = ex;
244.             }
245.             finally
246.             {
247.                 if (serviceCompletionNotifier != null)
248.                 {
249.                     // The Complete call will notify listeners that the service call is complete 
250.                     // and queued service requests can begin.
251.                     serviceCompletionNotifier.Complete(exception == null);
252.                 }
253.                 ViewModelBase.Log("ReactiveIndividualization::Complete");
254.             }
255. 
256.             // The callback is only used in the sample to show PlayReadyStatics within the UI on the 
257.             // completion of the ServiceRequest.
258.             if (callback != null)
259.             {
260.                 var dispatcher = CoreApplication.MainView.CoreWindow.Dispatcher;
261.                 await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
262.                 {
263.                     callback.Invoke();
264.                 });
265.             }
266.         }
267. 
268.         /// <summary>
269.         /// Reactive license acquisition is triggered by PlayReady when the DRM does not have a license for the 
270.         /// content(KeyID) as part of an active playback attempt.
271.         /// Service requests can be queued which is useful in scenarios such as root/leaf license and PlayReady Domains 
272.         /// where one license depends on another. The serviceCompletionNotifier is utilized to notify the ProtectionManager 
273.         /// that the next service request can begin.
274.         /// </summary>
275.         static async public void ReactiveLicenseAcquisition(IPlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest licenseRequest, 
276.                                                                    MediaProtectionServiceCompletion serviceCompletionNotifier,
277.                                                                    Action callback = null)
278.         {
279.             
280.             Exception exception = null;
281.             bool success = false;
282.             try
283.             {
284.                 await licenseRequest.BeginServiceRequest();
285.                 success = true;
286.             }
287.             catch (Exception ex)
288.             {
289.                 exception = ex;
290.             }
291.             finally
292.             {
293.                 if (serviceCompletionNotifier != null)
294.                 {
295.                     // The Complete call will notify listeners that the service call is complete
296.                     // and queued service requests can begin.
297.                     serviceCompletionNotifier.Complete(success);
298.                     serviceCompletionNotifier = null;
299.                 }
300.                 ViewModelBase.Log("ReactiveLicenseAcquisition::Complete");
301.             }
302. 
303.             // optional call back to update UI.
304.             if (callback != null)
305.             {
306.                 var dispatcher = CoreApplication.MainView.CoreWindow.Dispatcher;
307.                 await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
308.                 {
309.                     callback.Invoke();
310.                 });
311. 
312.             }
313.         }
314. 
315.         /// <summary>
316.         /// The individual process can kick off proactively and not need to wait for a reactionary service
317.         /// request from PlayReady. This enables the app to manage when activation occures.
318.         /// </summary>
319.         static public void ProactiveIndividualization(Action callback)
320.         {
321.             //Creating a new Indiv request and using the previous reactive method to complete the request 
322.             var indivRequest = new PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest();
323.             ReactiveIndividualization(indivRequest, null, callback);
324.         }
325. 
326.         /// <summary>
327.         /// The application can proativly request a license by creating a license request and 
328.         /// assigning a header which provides the specifics such as the KeyId of the license needed.
329.         /// </summary>
330.         static public void ProactiveLicenseAcquisition(PlayReadyContentHeader contentHeader, Action callback = null)
331.         {
332.             var laRequest = new PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest();
333.             laRequest.ContentHeader = contentHeader;
334.             ReactiveLicenseAcquisition(laRequest, null, callback);
335.         }
336. 
337. 
338. 
339. 
340.     }
341. }
